Time Model Statistics

  • DB Time represents total time in user calls
  • DB CPU represents CPU time of foreground processes
  • Total CPU Time represents foreground and background processes
  • Statistics including the word "background" measure background process time, therefore do not contribute to the DB time statistic
  • Ordered by % of DB time in descending order, followed by Statistic Name

  • % of Total Waits: value of .0 indicates value was <.05%; value of null is truly 0
  • Ordered by Event (only non-idle events are displayed)

9tby0zv97y2rf WITH msgs AS ( SELECT id, JSON_OBJECT( 'msg_group' VALUE msg_array, 'msg_text' VALUE sys.dbms_advisor.format_message_group(id) ) as msg FROM ( SELECT id, JSON_ARRAYAGG(JSON_OBJECT( 'msg_id' VALUE (fac || '-' || LPAD(TO_CHAR(message#), 5, '0')), 'seq' VALUE seq, 'p1' VALUE p1, 'p2' VALUE p2, 'p3' VALUE p3, 'p4' VALUE p4, 'p5' VALUE p5 ABSENT ON NULL) ORDER BY seq) msg_array FROM SYS.wri$_adv_message_groups WHERE task_id = :task_id GROUP BY id ) ) , pdb_stats AS ( SELECT s.con_dbid, SUM(CASE WHEN s.snap_id = :end_snap1 THEN (s.value / 1 000000) WHEN i.status = 'O' THEN 0 ELSE -(s.value / 1000000) END) as value FROM sys.awr_pdb_con_sys_time_model s, TABLE(prvt_hdm.get_cont_inst_cache(:top_cont_inst)) i WHERE s.dbid = :dbid1 AND ( s.instance_number IN (SELECT inst.instance_number FROM sys.dba_addm_instances inst WHERE inst.task_id = :task_id AND inst.status = 'ANALYZED' ) ) AND s.snap_id IN (:begin_snap1, :end_snap2) AND s.stat_name = :statname AND i.con_dbid = s.con_dbid AND i.inst_num = s.instance_number AND i.status IN ('N', 'O') GROUP BY s.con_dbid ) , final_stats AS ( SELECT s.con_dbid, p.pdb_name, p.cpus, s.value/:tp_secs1 as val_per_sec, s.value/(:tp_secs2 * p.cpus) as val_per_cpu FROM pdb_stats s, TABLE(prv t_hdm.get_cont_db_cache(:top_cont_db)) p WHERE s.con_dbid = p.con_dbid ) , top_by_value AS ( SELECT JSON_ARRAYAGG( JSON_OBJECT( 'dbid' VALUE con_dbid, 'name' VALUE pdb_name, 'cpus' VALUE cpus, 'stat' VALUE ROUND(val_per_sec, 2) ABSENT ON NULL ) ORDER BY val_per_sec DESC ) as x, COUNT(*) as cnt FROM ( SELECT * FROM (SELECT * FROM final_stats WHERE val_per_sec >= 0.1 AND pdb_name <> 'CDB$ROOT' ORDER BY val_per_sec DESC) WHERE ROWNUM <= :top1 UNION ALL SELECT * FROM final_stats WHERE val_per_sec >= 0.1 AND pdb_name = 'CDB$ROOT' ) ) , top_per_cpu AS ( SELECT JSON_ARRAYAGG( JSON_OBJECT( 'dbid' VALUE con_dbid, 'name' VALUE pdb_name, 'cpus' VALUE cpus, 'stat' VALUE ROUND(val_per_cpu, 2) ABSENT ON NULL ) ORDER BY val_per_cpu DESC ) as x FROM ( SELECT * FROM (SELECT * FROM final_stats WHERE val_per_sec >= 0.1 AND pdb_name <> 'CDB$ROOT' ORDER BY val_per_cpu DESC) WHERE ROWNUM <= :top2 UNION ALL SELECT * FROM final_stats WHERE val_per_sec >= 0.1 AND pdb_name = 'CDB$ROOT' ) ) SELECT JSON_OBJECT ( 'stat_type' VALUE :stattype, 'stat_spec' VALUE :statspec, 'stat_name' VALUE (SELECT m.msg FROM msgs m WHERE m.id = :statname_id), 'container_count' VALUE :containers, 'total_stat' VALUE ROUND(:total, 2), 'total_cpus' VALUE :total_cpus, 'title' VALUE (SELECT m.msg FROM msgs m WHERE m.id = :title), 'title_pc' VALUE (SELECT m.msg FROM msgs m WHERE m.id = :title_pc), 'data' VALUE top_by_value.x, 'data_pc' VALUE top_per_cpu.x ), top_by_value.cnt FROM top_by_value, top_per_cpu

PGA Aggr Target Stats

  • B: Begin Snap E: End Snap (rows dentified with B or E contain data which is absolute i.e. not diffed over the interval)
  • Auto PGA Target - actual workarea memory target
  • W/A PGA Used - amount of memory used for all Workareas (manual + auto)
  • %PGA W/A Mem - percentage of PGA memory allocated to workareas
  • %Auto W/A Mem - percentage of workarea memory controlled by Auto Mem Mgmt
  • %Man W/A Mem - percentage of workarea memory under manual control

  • Name suffixes: "(N)" - dispatcher started between begin and end snapshots "(R)" - dispatcher re-started between begin and end snapshots

Top SQL with Top Events

  • Top SQL statements by DB Time along with the top events by DB Time for those SQLs.
  • % Activity is the percentage of DB Time due to the SQL.
  • % Event is the percentage of DB Time due to the event that the SQL is waiting on.
  • % Row Source is the percentage of DB Time due to the row source for the SQL waiting on the event.
  • Executions is the number of executions of the SQL that were sampled in ASH.

  • Top SQL statements by DB Time along with the top row sources by DB Time for those SQLs.
  • % Activity is the percentage of DB Time due to the SQL.
  • % Row Source is the percentage of DB Time spent on the row source by that SQL.
  • % Event is the percentage of DB Time spent on the event by the SQL executing the row source.
  • Executions is the number of executions of the SQL that were sampled in ASH.

Top Blocking Sessions

  • Blocking session activity percentages are calculated with respect to waits on enqueues, latches and "buffer busy" only
  • '% Activity' represents the load on the database caused by a particular blocking session
  • '# Samples Active' shows the number of ASH samples in which the blocking session was found active.
  • 'XIDs' shows the number of distinct transaction IDs sampled in ASH when the blocking session was found active.

Activity Over Time

  • Analysis period is divided into smaller time slots as indicated in the 'Slot Time (Duration)'.
  • Top 3 events are reported in each of those slots
  • 'Slot Count' shows the number of ASH samples in that slot
  • 'Event Count' shows the number of ASH samples waiting for that event in that slot
  • '% Event' is 'Event Count' over all ASH samples in the analysis period

	  Findings and Recommendations
	  ----------------------------

Finding 1: Commits and Rollbacks
Impact is 4 active sessions, 90.86% of total activity.
------------------------------------------------------
Waits on event "log file sync" while performing COMMIT and ROLLBACK operations
were consuming significant database time.

  Top containers by Redo Generated in KB/sec
  ------------------------------------------
  DBID	       Container Name	Allocated  KB/sec   Percent
				CPUs		    of Total
  -----------  ---------------	---------  -------  --------
  3182349174   FREEPDB1 	2	   1928.79  99.19%
  1514744704   CDB$ROOT 	2	   16.02    .82%
  Average of all 2 containers:	2	   972.3    50%


  Top containers by Redo Generated in KB/sec per Allocated CPU
  ------------------------------------------------------------
  DBID	      Container Name  Allocated  KB/sec 	    KB/sec
			      CPUs	 per Allocated CPU
  ----------  --------------  ---------  -----------------  -------
  3182349174  FREEPDB1	      2 	 964.39 	    1928.78
  1514744704  CDB$ROOT	      2 	 8.01		    16.02


   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is 4 active sessions, 90.86% of total activity.
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Investigate application logic for possible reduction in the number of
      COMMIT operations by increasing the size of transactions.
   Rationale
      The application was performing 201137 transactions per minute with an
      average redo size of 593 bytes per transaction.

   Recommendation 2: Host Configuration
   Estimated benefit is 4 active sessions, 90.86% of total activity.
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Investigate the possibility of improving the performance of I/O to the
      online redo log files.
   Rationale
      The average size of writes to the online redo log files was 1 K and the
      average time per write was 0 milliseconds.
   Rationale
      The total I/O throughput on redo log files was 0 K per second for reads
      and 1.9 M per second for writes.
   Rationale
      The redo log I/O throughput was divided as follows: 0% by RMAN and
      recovery, 100% by Log Writer, 0% by Archiver, 0% by Streams AQ and 0% by
      all other activity.

   Symptoms That Led to the Finding:
   ---------------------------------
      Wait class "Commit" was consuming significant database time.
      Impact is 4 active sessions, 90.86% of total activity.


Finding 2: Log File Switches
Impact is .1 active sessions, 2.24% of total activity.
------------------------------------------------------
Log file switch operations were consuming significant database time while
waiting for checkpoint completion.
This problem can be caused by use of hot backup mode on tablespaces.
DML to tablespaces in hot backup mode causes generation of additional redo.

  Top containers by Redo Generated in KB/sec
  ------------------------------------------
  DBID	       Container Name	Allocated  KB/sec   Percent
				CPUs		    of Total
  -----------  ---------------	---------  -------  --------
  3182349174   FREEPDB1 	2	   1928.79  99.19%
  1514744704   CDB$ROOT 	2	   16.02    .82%
  Average of all 2 containers:	2	   972.3    50%


  Top containers by Redo Generated in KB/sec per Allocated CPU
  ------------------------------------------------------------
  DBID	      Container Name  Allocated  KB/sec 	    KB/sec
			      CPUs	 per Allocated CPU
  ----------  --------------  ---------  -----------------  -------
  3182349174  FREEPDB1	      2 	 964.39 	    1928.78
  1514744704  CDB$ROOT	      2 	 8.01		    16.02


   Recommendation 1: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .1 active sessions, 2.24% of total activity.
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Verify whether incremental shipping was used for standby databases.

   Recommendation 2: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .1 active sessions, 2.24% of total activity.
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Increase the size of the log files to 2048 M to hold at least 20 minutes
      of redo information.

   Symptoms That Led to the Finding:
   ---------------------------------
      Wait class "Configuration" was consuming significant database time.
      Impact is .1 active sessions, 2.29% of total activity.
